"tradfi news (@tradfi) ✧ | Tweeted | *SEC STAFF ISSUES FAQS ON CRYPTO ASSET SECURITIES LAWS APPLICATION

*SEC STAFF: TOKEN BUYBACKS ON FUNCTIONAL PROTOCOLS DO NOT CONSTITUTE MANAGERIAL EFFORTS

*SEC STAFF: LIQUID STAKING TOKENS ARE DIGITAL COMMODITIES OR TOOLS, NOT SECURITIES

*SEC STAFF: MAINTENANCE, ENHANCEMENTS, SYSTEM GRANTS NOT CONSIDERED ESSENTIAL MANAGERIAL EFFORTS

*SEC STAFF: PROMOTING CRYPTO UTILITY WITHOUT PROFIT CLAIMS GENERALLY NOT AN INVESTMENT CONTRACT"

[BK's message to you]
ELI5. Is this good or bad

**A:**

→ **The Short Version:** Massive green light. The SEC is basically stepping back and saying standard decentralized crypto mechanics don't automatically make something a security.

→ **What’s Clear:** Token buybacks on functional protocols, liquid staking tokens, routine network maintenance, and developer grants are officially treated as utility tools or commodities, not centralized investment contracts.

→ **Why It Matters:** It strips away years of weaponized ambiguity, giving builders and protocols actual legal breathing room without sweating an enforcement hammer for basic network functions.

→ **The Catch:** It’s staff-level guidance rather than a permanent congressional law, but it’s the clearest regulatory all-clear sign the space has gotten.

@Ling Mai (jynx_dsl) wants to know how to launder money through monero

**A:**

→ **Ring signatures** mix the actual sender's input with a pool of decoy signatures on the ledger, making the true origin mathematically obscured.

→ **Stealth addresses** generate a unique, one-off destination address for every transaction so the recipient's actual public key never touches the blockchain.

→ **RingCT (Ring Confidential Transactions)** hides the transfer amount entirely, preventing on-chain analysis from tracking volume or balances.
